
## 2026-11-14 compact pair-sum table and F = 8192 at 44-45 bits

### What was done
- Replaced the `HashMap<(BigUint, BigUint), ...>` pair-sum table by a compact open-addressing table (12-byte slots, hits verified); kept the old one as `build_pair_sum_table_exact`; new test `compact_pair_sum_table_matches_the_exact_table`; 52 `ec_index_calculus` tests pass; E34 H1 reproduces 72,132,515 trials (253 s against 271 s).
- Ran E44 H1, M44 H1, W44 at F = 8192 on new instances (seed `e222m221-mid-v2`): 2.57 GB each, 86 to 89 min each, obs/pred trials 0.990, 0.996, 1.000; IC/rho distance 1.1e4 to 2.2e4 (rho band). Data `evidence/mid44/f8192/`, write-up `RESULTS_mid44_f8192.md`.
### Decisions made
- Did not use RunPod: with the compact table F = 8192 fits locally (2.57 GB a run, three in parallel), and no RunPod credential or CLI is configured here.
### Open threads
- More instances per size (only one per family at this F); F above 8192 needs `PAIR_SUM_TABLE_MAX_FB` and `MAX_FB_SIZE` raised (13-bit indices); rho needs more than two runs per instance; independent replay, pcc claims, benchmark record, EKS.
### Errors
- The first test run's output was truncated to its last 25 lines, so the new test's name was not visible; ran it by name afterwards.
## 2026-11-14 — batched folded multi-target rho: the earlier IC lead was an artifact
### What was done
Lock-step batched folded multi-target rho (`prime_fast_j0_rho_multi.rs`, 128 lanes). 26 bit, same targets as the symmetric multi-target IC: rho/IC = 0.47 (T=20), 0.70 (T=200), 1.14 (T=1000); all targets verified in both arms. The unbatched comparison (rho/IC 1.06, 2.3, 5.5) was an artifact.
### Decisions made
No claim for the multi-target IC. Crossover near T≈1000 at 26 bit, trend in IC's favour with T.
### Open threads
Larger sizes (28-30 bit) where the crossover should move; tuned BSGS on j=0; §5a band; isolated rerun.
### Errors
Disk allowance ran out during a rebuild ("No space left on device"); freed 4.2 GB of validator build trees in the scratchpad (reports are in the repo) and rebuilt.

## PKM mixed-smooth-degree factor-base tooling (pkm-smooth-tower)

### What was done (commands, files, features)

Built tooling for Petit–Kosters–Messeng (PKC 2016) slides 12–17 with maps of
any small prime degree, where `ic_framework::prime_tower` only had degree 2:
`src/cryptanalysis/pkm_smooth_tower.rs` (Kummer form `x ↦ x^{q_j}` when
`S | p − 1`; isogeny form with odd/even-degree Vélu x-maps `N_j/D_j` on an
auxiliary curve with a cyclic subgroup of order `S = ∏ q_j`; Method 1
random-curve search recording curves tried; tower verifier; exact `m = 2`
relation finder checked by point addition; slide-17 system export in msolve
format with `S_{m+1}` as an `S_3` chain) and CLI `src/bin/pkm_smooth_tower.rs`
(`pkm-smooth-tower kummer|search|isogeny`). Unit tests in the module.

### Decisions made (and why)

`p` is always an input. The slide's "Method 2 (choose p yourself, a kind of
trapdoor)" is out of scope. Construction and checking only: no benchmark,
no ecbench plug-in and no claim [pcc:unverified], so benchmark-record and
resume rules do not apply. Conductor is not installed in this container
(as earlier entries record).

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Optional: wire the mixed tower into `ic_framework` as a factor-base plug-in
so ecbench can charge it, and a multi-step relation finder for `m ≥ 3`.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

None so far.

## PKM Method 2 (CM) for a given p added to pkm-smooth-tower

`cm_curves` (the 13 class-number-1 orders, every twist, order certified on
random points) and `cm_aux` (first CM curve with a cyclic smooth part ≥ the
target, fed to `isogeny_tower_on`); CLI `pkm-smooth-tower cm`. Tests: 6 of 6
pass (`cargo test --release --lib pkm_smooth_tower`), and the new test checks
CM orders against the generic count at 4 primes up to 998244353. Smoke run:
`cm --p 1000003 --bound 13 --min-size 300` built a verified tower on a
D = −28 curve, S = 1408 = 2^7·11; p = 1000033 and 1000039 had no fitting CM curve.

### Decisions made (and why)

Fixed-p only: the variant that chooses p so a CM order is smooth stays out of
scope. Class number 1 only. Hilbert polynomials (`isogeny_algos::cm`) for
larger class numbers would need root finding mod p and are left open.

Higher class numbers via `hilbert_class_poly` roots mod p.

None.

## Class number > 1 CM via Hilbert class polynomials

### What was done (commands, files, features)

`cm_curves_hilbert` in `src/cryptanalysis/pkm_smooth_tower.rs`: every
|D| ≤ M with h(D) ≤ H, Cornacchia filter, `isogeny_algos::cm::hilbert_class_poly`
reduced mod p, roots by Cantor–Zassenhaus (`poly_roots`), each twist certified
as p + 1 ∓ t. `cm_aux_from` generalises the tower handoff. CLI
`cm --max-disc M [--max-h H]`. Tests 8/8 pass. Recorded scans (|D| ≤ 1000,
h ≤ 16): p = 1000003, 1000033, 1000039 → 528, 782, 530 curves; 0 failed
polynomials, 0 incomplete splits, 0 rejected roots; towers D = −28 (S = 1408),
D = −99 (S = 891), D = −124 (S = 800). The last two primes had no
class-number-1 fit. Report, figure `cm_hilbert.svg` and PDF updated.

### Decisions made (and why)

Committed locally before tests finished, pushed only after 8/8 passed (PR has
auto-merge and its CI does not run these tests). Report states the tested
range rather than an untested |D| limit.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Best-fit instead of first-fit in `cm_aux_from`; the D = −7 skip at p = 1000003.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

A hand-typed curve count in the report (528 for p = 1000039) was wrong; checked
against the record and corrected to 530.

## EXP-PKM-ODD-001: odd/mixed and isogeny towers under F4, and the algebraic relation oracle

### What was done (commands, files, features)

User asked to settle "what is known": run F4 on odd/mixed-degree towers, add the auxiliary-curve
(isogeny) form (aux search uncharged, per user), and implement the slides' algebraic relation solver.
Added `examples/pkm_odd_tower_f4.rs` (generic `f4_fp`, Kummer and isogeny forms, tower/null, refute/planted,
exhaustive cross-check per row), `pkm_smooth_tower::{search_aux_exact, relation_system_polys,
algebraic_decompose, points_from_grevlex_basis, enumerate_decompositions}` and
`examples/pkm_algebraic_oracle.rs`. Unit tests 10/10 (oracle relation sets equal enumeration's). Probes:
A2 t=9 58 s; t=10 1,065 s and 10.8 GB (ladder capped at log2 S ≤ 9). Protocol frozen in fff5b8f before
any study row; run launched with `CHECKPOINT_SKIP=no-s3-credentials` (pcc bypass entry).

### Decisions made (and why)

`f4_fp::solve` stalled at p ≈ 2^30 (value-by-value fallback); replaced by a counted FGLM-style linear stage.
Local backend: no aws CLI/identity; metrics are counted. Director/executor relay not used (needs S3
benchmark records); disclosed in PROTOCOL §7.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Run in flight (`run_cells.sh`, resumable per cell); then `analyze.py`, report + PDF, tutorial §9 update.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

First 2^10 probe killed for memory while a build ran; re-probed alone. A shadowed variable broke the
first oracle build; fixed. Substitution solver stall handled as above.

## Descent-skip and lift-yield measurements (Claude)

### What was done
- Read the lift loop in `pkm_semaev.rs` and `cofactor_fiber` in `ic_framework/fiber.rs`: no explicit class skip exists; the fiber is generated from `[r]P` over the base points, so on `square-bits` it has 4 elements.
- Added an opt-in `descent_skip=1` flag (commit `1edbb0262c`), built `curve25519_family` (release, 9m59s, sha256 `ed99cb16...f99bd`) in the scratchpad, and ran the three registered cells x three arms. The flag fired 0 times; counts equal plain `lifts=1`. Reverted the flag from `src/` and kept it as `descent_skip.patch`.
- Part B (Python, 2,000 frames): one-bit class choice = 91.9% of the full-label choice, 2.0x random; parity of the coset label equals chi(x_M) on 1,547 of 1,547 points.
- Part C (2,000 targets) and C2 (20,000 targets, new seed, hypotheses frozen in A3): `lifts=1` costs 7.99x (`bits`) and 3.99x (`square-bits`); hits per 1e9 mul-adds: bits 0.099 -> 0.080, square-bits@0 0.187 -> 0.136, square-bits@414271 0.253 -> 0.169. Post hoc pooled rate ratio on `square-bits` 0.69, p = 0.0045; on `bits` 0.81, p = 0.63.

### Decisions made
- Kept the flag out of `src/` because it is a no-op. Reported P2 and P5 as falsified, H2 as unresolved.
- Added a post hoc `bits` control after the Part A result to check the fiber reading; labelled as such in PROTOCOL A1.

### Open threads
- Other bases (isogeny, edwards-bits, t0-bits, digits) not run in the engine; k, t, G only at 20, 5, 3. No rho arm: no distance to the matched rho.
- A larger-t or larger-k run would be needed to say anything about scaling.

### Errors
- First Poisson-interval script overflowed at 107 hits (floating `mu ** i`); fixed in log space, Part C table reproduced byte for byte.
- Three wait loops hit their time cap before the runs finished; rechecked the runs directly each time.
- `git fetch`/`checkout` was slow again; run in the background.

## 18:56 UTC — GHS strong-rho implementation and protocol frozen

### What was done (commands, files, features)

Added the isolated native study `research/quartic_ghs_strong_rho_20261114`
without modifying the frozen N4/N4-v2 sources. Its comparator implements the
negation quotient, 32 partitions, lockstep Montgomery batch inversion,
six-product cubic-field multiplication, deterministic cycle escape, complete
setup/walk/verification counters, and exact historical-target reconstruction.
The driver also retains frozen `old-neg`, emits interleaved calibration
records, and has separate `ic-repeat` and holdout-sequence policies.

Native self-tests matched the frozen field multiplication/inversion, point
addition, batched and pointwise inversion, and planted-log recovery at widths
1, 8, and 32. Development smoke runs at q = 223 verified both old and strong
rho on repeated exact IC targets [pcc:unverified]. The reconstructed `(ell,
k)` pairs at q = 223, 2,097,091, and 4,194,271 exactly matched the hydrated frozen raw archive
[pcc:unverified]. The archive's 382,105 bytes match its committed SHA-256
`aac9a25ce50ac5d23895f0dba8f907fb223aaac32a2a41391bc590e3c6bcca42`;
it was recovered from pre-migration commit
`e8743ca84185389926afca8390f5d17361958df9` and remains ignored.

Optimized the hot base-field product to use the family's safe u64 product,
used an addition chain for tiny extension constants, avoided unconditional
point negation, reused state hashes, and made cycle-comparison counters exact.
The final self-test passed under `rustc -C opt-level=3 -C target-cpu=native`.
`pod_run.sh` passed `bash -n` and freezes three concurrent single-thread seed
lanes with incremental IsoLab result upload.

Frozen `PROTOCOL.md` before any paid output. It fixes `strong-b64` as primary,
the k = 12..22 N4-v2 grid, seeds 1..3, exact IC targets, clustered sampling,
same-host repricing of both sides, the inherited three-weight native-work band,
admission gates, four bounded EKS splits, zero automatic retries, and a USD 5
hard ceiling. This entry records implementation controls only; no performance
measurement or leaderboard result was produced [pcc:unverified].

### Decisions made (and why)

Use b64 regardless of observed results; b32 and frozen old-neg are diagnostics
only. Compare historical IC and new rho only after both are re-priced with the
same EKS calibration. Keep wall time diagnostic under standard isolation and
make counted cold cost the primary result. A code/protocol PR does not change
the leaderboard because it contains no admitted measurement.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Review the diff, run repository/PCC gates, commit and merge the implementation
PR. From that merged revision, commit pinned EKS requests and a checkpoint,
perform budget/readiness preflight, launch the four finite splits, retrieve and
validate raw output, create PCC claims, render the report/PDF, and update every
leaderboard/dashboard/browser view in the same result PR if the measurement is
admitted.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

An initial self-test incorrectly required batching savings at width one; it was
corrected to apply only above width one. An early smoke target advanced past
the IC preamble; the interface now exposes a frozen `ic-repeat` policy, and
the low and high endpoint targets were checked against raw N4-v2 records. Tar
listing emitted repeated macOS xattr warnings, so subsequent extraction used
exact member names and suppressed only those listing warnings. No paid run or
leaderboard mutation occurred.

canonical minimum. All field, batch, curve, and planted-log self-tests still
pass [pcc:unverified].

(30 walks per width) and q = 16,363 (15 walks per width) over b1, b2, b4, b8,

cells and retained the expected negation-rho step scale across all three;
batch 4 was lower in the smaller q = 16,363 sample, while widths 16--64 lost
enough collision work to offset further inversion savings [pcc:unverified].
These development files remain under `/tmp` and are excluded from inference.

is now the fixed primary, b4/b16 are bracketing diagnostics, and each k =

accordingly. No EKS job, result claim, or leaderboard change occurred.

gate and prevented a knowingly weak b64 comparator from becoming the matched
reference.

- First read-out, from the Python prototype `analysis.py`:
  - Size law: beta is 1.96–1.97 for every arm and solver, inside the expected [1.9, 2.1]. The curvature test passes for U192@129 (coefficient interval spans 0).
  - Hold-out: passes on the six arms that reached 2^20. It is "not evaluable" for the U192@129 target, which timed out at 2^20.

- Finish the Rust port and cross-check. Then the report/PDF, benchrec close, progress report and the PR.

on all of V); `scripts/raw_closure.py` (raw polynomial-calculus closure, five
presentations K/S/T/M/C); `AMENDMENTS.md` A1 (arms M, C added after the K/S/T
smoke test, predictions recorded before running them); `scripts/run_grid.sh`
(grid running in the background, `runs/progress.txt`).

### Decisions made (and why)

Treat PKM's two map-finding methods as one object (Kummer tower of `G_m` or
of the norm-one torus `T₂`) and test presentation invariance of the solving
degree, because the degree staircase, not the map search, is what closes the
lane; the aux-curve search can only be a constant. Toy-scale stage diagnostic
only, `[pcc:unverified]`, no `S`, no leaderboard row. The result-generating
closure is being ported to a standalone Rust crate (`closure/`, in progress by
a delegated agent) per AGENTS.md "Implementation language: no Python"; the
Python closure stays as the independent cross-check, as in L1.

## 19:55 UTC — PKM torus towers: grid finished, result written (PR #4153)

### What was done (commands, files, features)

Native closure crate `research/pkm_torus_tower_20261114/closure/` (`pc_closure`,
Rust, serde_json only; built by a delegated Opus agent, 16 s build) replaces the
Python closure as the result generator; the Python closure stays as the
independent cross-check on identical dumped systems: 41 cells agree on the
certified degree and on every per-degree rank and expansion count, 7 agree on
the degrees Python finished before its memory cap, 0 disagree. Native grid
(`scripts/run_grid_native.sh`, 99 cells, 713 s total, column budget 45,000):
raw PC degree, two seeds per cell, five presentations — K: m=2 4,4,4,4,4,5,5
(t=1..7), m=3 5,5,6; S and T (Möbius doubling): m=2 4,5,5,5,5,5,5, m=3 5,6,6;
M and C (polynomial tower + bilinear link): m=2 4,4,5,5,5,5, m=3 5,5,6 (C m=3
t=2 only). T replicated at p=73727 (t=2..5: 5,5,5,5). Planted controls never
reached 1 (D ≤ 6–14). Positive control: K reproduces the L1 ladder at every
D ≥ 4. `[pcc:unverified]` toy stage diagnostic. README, `results.csv`,
`results_table.md`, `figures/d_vs_n.svg`, `report.pdf` written. Earlier the
first commit swept `closure/target/` into git and the `migrate` CI job lost a
push race on it; fixed by untracking it and adding `closure/.gitignore`. The
Python grid was stopped by hand once the native crate existed (five concurrent
dense Python cells exhausted the 15 GB host).

### Decisions made (and why)

Frozen prediction P1 (presentation invariance) is REFUTED, replicated: the
Möbius-coordinate presentations cost one degree from t = 2, the polynomial
tower + bilinear link presentations from t = 3, and all rejoin the Kummer value
at m=2 t=6–7 and m=3 t=3 — a shift of the staircase, not a new law. The PKM
exponent is unchanged; the gain is the removed auxiliary-curve search phase
(constant-level accounting, NIST primes have v₂(p+1) = 64/96/521) and the
answer to the open C6 question (supersingular shortcut usable at ≤ 1 degree
cost in these cells; present T₂ as the circle, never as x-maps). No leaderboard
row, no `S`, no benchmark record (no pipeline was priced).

## 23:19 UTC — local full-image build exposed container trust mismatch

### What was done (commands, files, features)

Ran the complete pinned Docker build against the clean exact-merge context.
The base image and 72.23-MB repository context loaded, then the first HTTPS
runtime-tool download stopped before copying or running the experiment.

### Decisions made (and why)

Move the already hash-pinned jq and AWS CLI downloads into
`build_context.sh`, where the managed host's trusted TLS path is available,
and copy the hash-checked bytes into the Docker build. Keep certificate
verification enabled; do not add `curl -k`, bake a local interception CA into
the release image, or weaken the existing SHA-256 checks. This also makes the
Docker layer network-independent after context assembly.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Patch the context/image recipe, rebuild a fresh context, and rerun the full
image/toolchain check.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

Container-local curl rejected the managed Envoy path with `curl: (60) SSL
certificate problem: self-signed certificate in certificate chain`. Host curl
reached both official HTTPS endpoints through Envoy with certificate
verification intact. The failed layer created no image and launched no job.

## 23:21 UTC — hash-checked tool staging passed; VFS layer expansion blocked image

### What was done (commands, files, features)

The revised context builder downloaded both official artifacts with TLS and
matched all four pinned hashes. It produced a clean 149.64-MB Docker context
for the exact #4171 merge. The next Docker build consumed that context and
reached the hash-checked-tool installation layer.

### Decisions made (and why)

Collapse the context copies and filesystem setup into one COPY plus one RUN.
The local Docker daemon uses the VFS driver, so each filesystem layer copies
the roughly 1.6-GB base root; separate jq, AWS CLI, repository, verification,
and user-creation layers are needlessly expensive. A single controlled context
still preserves every hash and exact-revision check while making both this
runtime and ordinary overlay builders leaner.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Apply the layer collapse, remove only this session's failed build cache, and
retry the full build.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

The second build failed before executing its RUN with `no space left on
device` while VFS prepared another full root copy. This is a storage-driver
layer-expansion failure, not a hash, compiler, or experiment failure.

## 23:37 UTC — admission logic hardened and exact image self-tested

### What was done (commands, files, features)

Hardened `make_requests.py` so all three named budget readbacks carry period,
limit, actual spend, reserved spend, and remaining balance; the generator now
recomputes the effective headroom as their minimum and rejects duplicate,
stale, arithmetically inconsistent, exhausted, or sub-USD-5 receipts. Hardened
`analyze.py` so source hashes bind exact study paths, seed/final exits occur
exactly once at rc=0, the low replay exit is explicit, and H1 classification
uses only admitted sizes while allowing an admitted unresolved/rejected cell
to override a separate unadmitted size. Thirteen focused tests pass.
[pcc:unverified]

Moved pinned jq/AWS CLI acquisition into the host-trusted context builder,
collapsed the image to one BuildKit filesystem layer, compressed the sparse
Git index, and made the request enter `/opt/crypto` explicitly with GNU
`env --chdir`. This fixes the original script's implicit-current-directory
failure without changing its frozen bytes. The Dockerfile now compiles and
runs the scientific self-test while building.

The full linux/amd64 image build completed as local image
`sha256:b2d99ad703391d5820bef8ef8496a0a2b89bd9c901b3dc0df61b749e9793cd7f`
from exact merge `352dd51d…d486`. Inside the build, both tool hashes, Git

1.7.1, and AWS CLI 2.40.3 passed. This is a local packaging check, not a
published ECR digest or performance measurement. [pcc:unverified]

### Decisions made (and why)

Use build-time compilation/self-test as the local container qualification.
The VFS daemon needs a full 1.87-GB root copy merely to create a runtime
container; the image build already executes the same compiler/self-test under
the pinned root and avoids weakening read-only pod settings. Keep `pod_run.sh`
unchanged and put the required repository cwd in every hashed request.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Remove only the temporary local image/context/cache, run the required release
library and repository gates, update this log with exact outcomes, then
checkpoint, commit, push, and open the normal auto-merge PR.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

Two intermediate VFS builds exhausted local disk while materializing extra
filesystem snapshots, and a post-build `docker run` could not create another
full VFS root. The recipe was reduced to one layer and the isolated worktree's
sparse checkout was narrowed without changing Git status. A final clean build
with its embedded self-test passed; no cloud resource or primary sample was
created.

  candidates. Fixed: a probe whose baby steps are not pairwise distinct is discarded (up to 8 probes), so the scan is
  complete; the test now also uses primes where the Weil window holds a single multiple of the exponent.

- `benchrec.py init` rejected a free-text `--problem`; used the stable key `hcdlp:genus_ladder`.

## 21:55 PST — n7 membership finite results and density falsifier (Codex)

### What was done
- Rust direct and projective W1 membership sets matched exactly at q=2 and q=4 for both cubic Frobenius factors. Counts per cubic were 42 and 2898; unions were 56/63 and 3906/4095 of the nonzero relative-trace-zero populations. The amended arm constructs Frobenius kernels by binary Gaussian elimination, avoiding full-field setup in the candidate test.
- Froze Amendment 2 and an S3 pre-run checkpoint before q=8 direct enumeration. The observed union was 237839/262143; its complement 24304 falsified the q=2,4-derived formula `(q-1)(q^3-1)`, whose q=8 prediction was 3577. Native q=8 direct run took 26.57 seconds wall including build. See `research/ghs_n7_membership_20261114/reports/`.
- Checked the conditional projective-scaling theorem with Lean 4.35.0. Wrote the evidence-linked Markdown report, SVG, and three-page PDF, visually inspected the PDF, and ran `pcc lint-prose` with zero findings. PR #4261 remains the result PR.

### Decisions made
- Retain the falsified density formula as a negative result. Label the projective method as a candidate method for a known published family. The Lean file does not prove the Frobenius-kernel dimension, trace lemma, or GHS transfer; the report states those gaps explicitly [pcc:unverified].

### Open threads
- Independent membership implementation and prior-work check; general trace lemma and sharper exact family-size bounds; concrete subgroup-preserving Hess map; complete same-subgroup transfer and rho costs; search for a distinct eligible family.
